People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I mean… I lose track of what I’m doing sometimes, but I haven’t noticed, like, forgetting recent things in a clear way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. It’s more like I can’t sit on an idea. I started trying to line up new commissions, messaging old customers, planning a website, sketching a whole Appalachian collection… all at once. Like it all had to happen right then.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes. I don’t really think of it that way. I just get moving on something and don’t always stop to sort it out first.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with family or friends because of acting on impulse or not thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah… some. My family’s been checking on me more lately. I guess they got worried because I wasn’t keeping up with my usual stuff.\n\nAnd work got messy too. I missed a big custom delivery, and my shop records got so disorganized I had to stop taking new work for now. So… yeah, it’s caused problems.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel overwhelmed or stressed when you're unable to focus or complete tasks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. That’s honestly kind of what brought me in.\n\nAt the bench, I’ll try to work on one order, and then I’m checking a customer email, then staring at some saw mark, then I hear something outside… and by the time I look back, I’ve lost track of the whole work order. It gets pretty overwhelming.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're constantly on the go or like you can't slow down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. At the bench I couldn’t really sit still. I’d start sketching a watch case, then jump to emailing suppliers, then rearrange the display trays… even after closing. Just kind of kept going.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have mood swings?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess after the show especially. I’d feel really up, like… bigger than myself, and then if someone told me to slow down or keep normal shop hours, I’d snap at them. Not proud of it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

It’s gotten pretty bad. I missed a big custom delivery, and my shop records are such a mess I had to stop taking new work for now. I mean… I lose track of what I’m doing sometimes, but I haven’t noticed, like, forgetting recent things in a clear way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of.
